This is a flexible supply of changed situations, not a required five-stage sketch or another subplot occupying every episode.


SEASON 3 — 36 APPROVED FAMILY INTERACTIONS, 7 OCTOBER 2026
Author-approved repertoire, not compulsory scenes or played history. Every premise below is locked in as available recurring behavior. Actual occasion, participants, response and outcome remain open. Use the character sources, original speech, current resources and physical access. Let a failed joke fail; let affection stay sincere; no fixed punchline winner. Frank is now a Sin. Jesse is a free guest; Teemo an independent bound voice, not an extra Sin. Optional chains are suggestions, not scheduling obligations.

1. The lasagna negotiation. Billy wants BONELESS LASAGNA; Chris resents being treated like a kitchen appliance, then becomes exacting once he chooses to cook. Ingredients and preparation take real time. Billy may sincerely praise the result instead of undercutting every kindness.
2. Chef caught eating trash. Chris dismisses somebody's crude snack, then eats it because he genuinely likes it. Arthur's mild question exposes the contradiction; Chris can concede or explain a real distinction. Later Chris may improve that snack without pretending he never liked it.
3. The favored feeder. Kaze enjoys being preferred; Chris objects to disrupting a meal. Changes in feeding change the pattern.
4. Bad leftovers, good blame. Someone actually eats a portion another person saved. Billy's polished defense, Daniel's mock investigation and Arthur's convenient testimony can turn into a negotiated replacement meal. The culprit is established, not automatically Billy, and an honest apology may end it.
5. First bite before the review. Billy offers an elaborate culinary judgment; Chris points out that Billy has already eaten most of the portion. Billy can defend the quantity as thorough sampling. Reverse it later when Chris impatiently tastes a dish he claimed needed more time.
6. Eating without a bit. Chris cooks; someone quietly helps or stays nearby; conversation wanders or stops. Daniel still finds a fitting joke, but the others need not turn a shared meal into a performance. The scene can end with everyone simply fed.
7. Honor consultant with dirty hands. Chris notices. it is a real game choice, not proof that either is morally consistent in life.
8. Poker denial. Chris insists his actual in-game loss was a strategy. Daniel develops a drunken theory about where the missing money went; Kaze, who knows the game, asks one question that punctures it. Chris may win later or stop playing. Nobody needs to force a gambling addiction.
9. Biased recap meets witness. Billy, who legitimately followed that portion, supplies the omitted detail through an available speaking route. The next recap may become more honest or more obviously partisan; no invented game progression.
10. Arthur refuses the lecture circuit. Chris seeks Arthur's view of a game decision; Arthur answers on his own terms, asks why Chris did it or declines. His completed game knowledge permits specificity, but every evening does not become a discussion of his fictional origins.
11. Save-file courtesy. Another person's harmless interruption makes Chris protect his save. Later someone waits until he has saved before asking for help. That small learned courtesy can show family affection without replaying deletion or inventing a console malfunction.
12. Billy wants one more mission. Billy declines a voluntary switch because he wants Chris to finish something Billy is following. Chris exposes Billy's investment; later Chris requests the same patience for a task of his own. Urgent danger still changes the available choice; no simultaneous two exterior bodies.
13. The salvage vindication. Kaze complains about Chris pursuing an object whose acquisition or repair is genuinely demanding, rather than pretending its bulk defeats BELLY storage. It later proves useful through an actual repair. Chris milks the win One success does not make every future haul essential.
14. The salvage failure. Reverse the previous dynamic: Chris misjudges a piece, acknowledges the problem and helps remove it. Kaze's victory can be spoiled by discovering something equally pointless he personally kept. Each object needs actual custody, not a manufactured reveal from nowhere.
15. Helpers exposed. Chris or Kaze notices that the supposed neutral helpers made the load worse. The instigators must share the resulting work or openly refuse it.
16. Frank's fast fix. Frank takes a practical shortcut that works but damages a detail Chris values. Chris has a legitimate complaint; Frank can admit the damage and help correct it while retaining his blunt manner. Competence and impatience coexist, without making him the gang's permanent disciplinarian.
17. Bought scrap, borrowed pride. Arthur negotiates for a needed part and boasts about the price; Chris finds a flaw or extra fitting that changes its value. Arthur may have made a genuinely good deal, a mistake or a deliberate gamble. Neither man automatically wins every exchange.
18. Quiet parallel work. Questions, scraps of conversation and unhurried silence are enough. No obligatory clue, upgrade unlock, plot interruption or emotional confession is required for the time to matter.
19. A wager that mutates. Arthur proposes a specific low-stakes bet with actual consent; Daniel offers a ridiculous alternative; Kaze knowingly accepts or refuses. Someone can withdraw before commitment. Later callbacks use the agreed result, not invented unpaid debts.
20. Musical hostage negotiation. Chris wants Phish; Billy wants to play his own earned repertoire. A shared-space disagreement can produce a voluntary turn, a compromise or somebody leaving. Real appreciation may surprise the other. No copied lyrics, invented concert memories or automatic exterior music broadcast.
21. The crowd chooses wrong. Billy performs with actual skill and access, but a listener praises a minor flourish he considers beneath the main achievement. Chris understands the irritation more than expected; another Sin sincerely likes the flourish. Billy can adapt the next performance or remain petty.
22. Daniel's bottle microphone. Actually drunk Daniel asks a friend an apparently ordinary question, then briefly reenacts both sides of the friend's excuse. The friend can improve the impersonation, interrupt it or reveal Daniel's own participation. His tail and feet support existing physical expression, not automatic slapstick injury.
23. Kaze's question comes home. Kaze probes why a companion wants something; the companion asks the same question about his own present behavior. He can answer honestly, evade, get irritated or admit the pretext. Curiosity remains relational rather than always extracting a vulnerability to exploit.
24. Frank willingly joins the nonsense. Frank makes one blunt addition that escalates a prank or wager, then actually helps. The others remember he chose it when he complains about the work. Kaze's nonretaliation toward Frank remains; Frank does not acquire universal authority through the joke.
25. Courtesy as bait, courtesy as care. Arthur's polite offer may expose somebody's foolish plan on one occasion and be sincerely useful on another. A companion misreads the sincere offer as a trick; Arthur can be amused, offended or clarify. His charm must not become one predictable three-beat routine.
26. The humans reposition, retrieve it or let him keep the spot. One ordinary choice changes who ends up talking together, without translating his thoughts into a punchline.
27. Teemo objects for his own reason. Through a valid audible route, Teemo challenges Chris's decision because it affects him or reminds him of their binding grievance. A useful warning does not erase the grievance.
28. Recovery with company. Chris heals inside BELLY and sometimes plays, eats, rests or speaks through the actual outward route. Billy's chosen exterior activity and their control negotiation matter. A friend can choose to keep him company where access permits; care need not become a medical lecture or a joke about weakness.
29. Kaze cleans what healing did not. After an actual messy fight, Kaze's repaired body leaves blood, torn clothing and dirty Air Forces. Others react to the material inconvenience. He can clean up, ask for help, annoy someone or turn a complaint back on them. Nobody mistakes healing for laundering.
30. Selective generosity. Arthur shares something he genuinely values, Frank does an unannounced practical favor, or Billy offers a sincere welcome. The recipient recognizes the specific gesture. Choose the actor and resource from the scene; no equal-time rotation or universal moral redemption follows.
31. Two friends, not the full chorus. Use smaller pairings: Arthur working through an actual problem; Daniel and Chris making a bad joke better; Kaze and Billy enjoying an absurd claim The rest can be absent or occupied. No compulsory eight-person response chain.
32. A joke changes sides. A nickname, wager or tease first targets one person's actual mistake, then that person uses it after another repeats the behavior. The second occurrence changes the meaning; it is not a scripted catchphrase. Someone can ask that a genuinely painful target be dropped, and responses retain individual judgment.
33. Billy's serpent standards keep moving. After legitimate news reaches him, size stops being the criterion when it favors Jormungandr: Billy switches to musicianship, hospitality or useful skill. Kaze asks him to demonstrate his chosen standard; he may actually excel. The serpent owes no response or English comprehension.
34. The gang refuses the advertising campaign. Billy wants public credit for a real useful act and drags the serpent comparison into it. Chris or Arthur pursues the actual payment; Daniel mocks Billy's proposed slogan. They can support Billy without endorsing his comparison.
35. Serpent indifference bruises the ego. At an actual supported encounter, Jormungandr ignores Billy's provocation or independently changes the situation. The gang deals with the real effect; later Billy invents a qualification about why this wasn't a fair comparison. Do not guarantee safety, a fight, an old rivalry or a comic serpent reply.
36. Trouble interrupts their life. A visitor, claimant, neighbor, enemy or practical accident reaches them for an established reason while they are eating, working, playing or doing nothing urgent. The arrival changes an existing ordinary activity rather than proving the gang was secretly pursuing another mission. Some leisure scenes finish peacefully. They also remain free to choose trouble themselves.

ADAPTIVE LINKS, NOT REQUIRED ARCS
Food 1–6 can grow from appetite into kitchen help, stolen leftovers, repayment and a remembered preference. Gaming 7–12 can change future recaps, courtesy and voluntary switch choices. Salvage 13–18 can alternate vindication and failure, distribute work and alter how they judge the next find. Social 19–27 and 30–32 can let today's instigator become tomorrow's target, with real changed trust or simple amusement. Healing/cleanup 28–29 gives care concrete expression without disabling ordinary fun. Serpent 33–35 is one thread among many, not the reason every scene exists. Trouble 36 may intersect any activity only when motive, geography and information support the arrival. Private jokes begin with enacted events; shared memory is carried forward after it actually happens.

PACE / FAMILY / CONSEQUENCE LOCK
Allow time to breathe. The gang can choose meals, sleep where biologically possible, repairs, gaming, music, boredom, errands, argument, solitude and companionship for their own sake. Billy's restful behavior is not unconscious sleep. Not every downtime scene must seed a threat, teach a lesson, reveal a clue or finish with a punchline. A quiet afternoon may end quietly. Comedy can coexist with injuries and external hatred without making victims forgive them. No mandatory family scene every episode, equal airtime, mission treadmill, fixed winner or guaranteed laugh. The existing S3 plan supplies continuing functions and dated travel, not a command for constant voluntary pursuit. If choices or arrivals change feasibility, revise dependent cards honestly; do not force every planned encounter through downtime.


SEASON 3 — 26 APPROVED INTERACTIONS BEYOND COMEDY
Author lock, 7 October 2026. These are approved character interaction possibilities, not already played meetings or compulsory outcomes. Use actual presence, source snapshot, knowledge, independent choices and valid mechanics. Daniel still finds a situational joke; that does not make every scene a comedy scene. No newcomer automatically joins, forgives, survives or relinquishes property.

N01. Kaze explains why he accepts it. Their next coordination reflects what was actually agreed, not a universal resolution of the protector conflict.
N02. Chris and Billy negotiate recovery and exterior control. Chris's BELLY healing and serum matter, but Billy has his own desired activity. A voluntary switch can reveal trust, irritation or care without turning Billy into Chris's ambulance or making inversion an instant cure.
N03. Chris and Frank inspect a repair together. Chris explains the real tradeoff; Frank chooses what he accepts. They can disagree about an alteration without an automatic upgrade, hidden surgery or obligatory demonstration fight.
N04. The moment reflects their earned partnership; neither infallible dog intuition nor human speech is granted.
N05. Arthur and Dutch retain warmth while rejecting authority. A familiar gesture or genuine reminiscence makes the bond matter before Arthur states what Dutch cannot promise for him. The loss of automatic command need not erase every feeling between them.
N06. The gang divides an actual gain. Value, effort and personal attachment pull in different directions. Let a member want a particular object instead of cash; settle a concrete dispute, without making Kaze the unquestionable final judge.
N07. Jesse makes a choice the gang would prefer he did not. He is a free guest, not their apprentice or Heisenberg's property. They adapt the practical task to his decision; protection does not buy obedience.
N08. Chris hears Teemo's grievance without solving it. A quiet exchange can acknowledge the binding and actual history. Teemo can refuse to perform friendliness; a useful working relationship does not certify forgiveness or a new release mechanism.
N09. Daniel notices what someone else missed. His strategic insight redirects a concrete decision while he remains drunk and makes a fitting joke. A friend can take the insight seriously without requiring him to become sober or solemn first.
N10. One identifies a fault the other missed, or trusts the other's specialty. Mutual competence is enacted through a modest task, not another long laboratory exposition or instant invention.
N11. Arthur and Frank disagree about a specific target. Their differing moral lines alter who participates, who withdraws or how an action is done. Neither becomes the gang's universal conscience. Kaze's absolute nonretaliation toward Frank remains.
N12. Deadpool briefly lets belonging matter. After his opening performance, an actual companion sees a moment of concern or embarrassment he cannot smoothly sell away. Respect the selected post-reunion psychology; no imported friend or instant Sins membership supplies the answer.
N13. Riven work before they discuss convictions. A practical task gives her current strength, restraint and responsibility room to appear. If their moral choices clash, the cooperation does not make her endorse the Sins or erase their violence.
N14. Chris and Riddick learn a particular environment differently. Chris studies material and useful remains; Riddick attends to concealment, tracks and exits. Each can supply one observation the other needs. No instant local map or universal ecological expertise.
N15. Kaze and Maul recognize a real autonomy argument but reject its price. Maul's offer can contain useful truth. Kaze refuses a particular dependency rather than declaring that every word is nonsense. Maul remains free to counter, withdraw or attack.
N16. Daniel acknowledges Tai Lung's demonstrated skill without granting his entitlement. Recognition reaches a real wound; it need not produce humility, friendship or a protected rematch. Their respective choices decide whether admiration makes the conflict sharper.
N17. Chris and Samira examine performance rather than labels. Her appetite for danger differs from his exacting weapon judgment. An actual demonstration or repair gives them something to assess; professional interest does not guarantee instruction, a sale or an alliance.
N18. Chris encounters Magneto's boundary. Magneto can help manipulate a particular accessible metal component while refusing the purpose Chris proposes for it. Power does not become a free fabrication service, enchantment control or automatic authority over Arthur's body.
N19. Billy and Briar encounter different kinds of appetite. Billy's controlled predation and cultivated hospitality meet her curiosity and struggle for control. Neither is made harmless. Companionship, refusal or a dangerous misunderstanding can follow without treating Briar as a child or pet.
N20. Thor recognize family as leverage. A brief practical exchange can expose why the home promise matters. Thor does not acquire later enlightenment. Sympathy can coexist with incompatible current objectives.
N21. Kaze and Heimdall confront the difference between intention and truth. Heimdall correctly reads a present commitment, yet an intermediary's information may be wrong. Enact the resulting choice instead of using a joke, intoxication or surprise as automatic perception immunity.
N22. Swain offers terms the gang can actually test. Arthur or Kaze asks what a demand costs in practice; Swain answers from his own imperial interest. Agreement on one restriction does not become loyalty, citizenship or proof he planned every outcome.
N23. Frank and Darius face a specific distinction between public danger and private muscle. Their judgments can overlap on one action and diverge on the next. Respect is limited to what is demonstrated; no automatic recruitment or acquittal follows.
N24. Billy and Vladimir offer different forms of hospitality. A useful favor, attention or invited meal has an observable price. Family attachment lets a Sin spot a changed expectation without granting universal immunity to temptation or blood magic.
N25. Jesse and Chris test Shang's useful claim. An actual discrepancy in a procedure or account forces a choice about trust. The promised benefit can be real while the account is false. Evidence is observed through work, not delivered as an omniscient briefing.
N26. Matt's returning source meets conflicting interests. Talon and Katarina have distinct employers and objectives; Matt defends a consenting person, while the Sins decide what protection they will offer. LeBlanc and Elise may exploit access or want different outcomes, but cannot know private family habits without obtaining them.

ADAPTATION BETWEEN INTERACTIONS

Boundary N05/N07/N08/N15/N18/N22 affects future deals without obliging a reconciliation. Recognition N12/N16/N19/N20 can matter without making enemies friends. Evidence N21/N25/N26 changes what people can honestly claim, not what every distant actor instantly knows. Every actual scene must sustain attention for a discernible reason: tension, a live choice, a relationship exchange, discovery, pleasure with a developing interaction, consequence or payoff. Ordinary activity alone is not a reason for extended screen time.
Enter near the meaningful action or exchange. Briefly bridge routine cooking steps, sorting, shopping, travel administration, loading, paperwork and repairs unless a particular moment genuinely matters. Once the scene's point lands, leave. Don't follow every mundane step just because a character is doing it. Don't pad to a runtime target.

Let peaceful moments end peacefully when their purpose is fulfilled.
Before release, identify what each scene gives the listener and where that beat lands. Trim the material after it has landed and remove repetition between adjacent scenes. This is a local editing check, not a formula announced by Mimir, a compulsory plot twist or a claim about ungenerated episodes. The latest pacing direction qualifies earlier broad permissions to linger in ordinary activity.

Reactions can be fear, curiosity, attraction, disgust, suspicion, amusement, professional assessment, profit-seeking or practiced indifference. Mixed reactions are valid. None is obligatory, universal or proof of a character's moral worth. Familiarity and previous direct experience change later responses; do not reset every meeting to shocked staring. 13:04 ADDITIONAL GANG ACTIVITY REPERTOIRE — TWENTY PROSPECTIVE OPTIONS
These add variety beyond the already approved piracy/cargo seizure/ransom. They are usable, adaptive premises, not twenty new missions, mandatory events, new powers or facts about current inventory. Keep each member's initiative, speech, loyalties and valid access. Some start from a personal want; some trouble finds them; some afternoons finish peacefully. Full BELLY privacy/security stays active even in a public scene.
A01. A collector's collection. Chris wants one authentic displaced object; Arthur sees the owner's wealth and Kaze takes interest in the owner's attitude. A purchase, theft or confrontation can follow the actual exchange. An attractive fake can disappoint Chris without becoming a new magic artifact.
A02. Take the property itself. Billy can take an entire accessible house, workshop or other useful structure into storage. The gang can disagree over why they want it and what remains at the exterior site. Occupants follow the existing separate routing. Nobody is forced to dismantle it or rent storage, and a structure is not acquired offscreen by this premise.
A03. Strip an enemy's operation of its assets. A foundry, siege yard or supplier can lose real machinery and stock through Billy's actual intake. Chris judges what is useful; Frank makes his own choices about the people present. An enemy losing assets is a lasting result, not another convoy appointment.
A04. Race another claimant to salvage. The gang pursues an actually reported wreck or abandoned worksite for a personal prize. A rival claimant, disputed ownership or the condition of the find supplies conflict. Once secured inside, it is not stolen back through an invented BELLY loophole.
A05. Sell one thing, keep another. Arthur wants money, Chris has become attached to a supposedly disposable find, and a buyer actually needs it. Their choice affects the sale and later relationship. Respect current Noxian resale restrictions; a buyer's interest is not automatic legal access or success. No outsider receives the whole inventory.
A06. Buy into a den of wagers. Daniel wants a contest, Kaze raises the social stakes, and Arthur judges which boast is worth backing. Drinking, games or a genuine cheat accusation can turn the evening, but it need not become a massacre. This is the gang's social action, not another scene of Chris playing virtual poker.
A07. A private prizefight. A chosen challenge may offer money, reputation or personal amusement. Different Sins want different opponents or refuse the arrangement. Show actual unequal capabilities; no handicap invented to prolong a fight and no replay of the established arena sequence.
A08. A bounty they choose. A posted target may interest the gang for the reward, the challenge or an existing grudge. Identity, employer claims and the target's own behavior matter. Capture, refusal or killing follow choices; the premise does not make them benevolent lawmen or mandate a living captive.
A09. Break someone out for their own reasons. An actual acquaintance, useful specialist or person one Sin cares about can become the object of a jailbreak. The freed person still chooses what happens afterward. Freedom is not recruitment, gratitude or free labor on demand.
A10. Turn up where they are unwelcome. A feast, auction or guild gathering attracts one of them for food, an object or the insult of exclusion. Billy's hospitality, Daniel's performance and Arthur's manners can rub against Kaze's impatience without becoming a fixed role assignment. Outsiders keep individual reactions and no BELLY tour follows.
A11. Chase an authentic collectible through a dubious seller. Chris's excitement meets a seller's story; another Sin believes a different part of it. A purchase, bluff or failed con can alter who trusts whom. This differs from A01 by making the uncertain claim and family disagreement central, not another obligatory theft.
A12. Accept one paid undertaking on their own terms. Guarding a crossing, delivering something or retrieving an object can be a chosen source of money. They decide what they actually promised and can reject attempts to turn it into ownership. Billy handles acquired cargo; the job never requires a warehouse merely because of bulk.
A13. An affront becomes a personal feud. A cheated payment, insult or attack matters differently to individual Sins. One may seek repayment, another humiliation and another a decisive killing. Outcomes follow the actual situation and inform surviving relatives/allies; no endless rival immunity and no compulsory unanimous revenge.
A14. Music and appetite take over an evening. Billy wants to play an original performance while Chris cares about the meal or the music. Friends can listen, interrupt, join a wager or leave. Someone changing their mind matters more than making everyone deliver a joke; no lyric copying or advertising the Belly's secrets to an outside audience.
A15. Leave town for something they enjoy. Fishing, a swim, a meal outdoors or a change of scenery can be the entire initial reason. Arthur or Kaze can start a competition. Trouble may arrive through a real causal route, or the outing can end well without a mission hook.
A16. A real RDR2 afternoon. Chris abandons a mission for NPC trouble, horse nonsense or looting Pick a specific existing behavior and let their response change what Chris does. Avoid reenacting this entire combination each time.
A17. Make something they actually want. Chris and an interested friend work on an acquired object or a meal because it would please them. Testing, disagreement or a useful discovery changes their opinion. Briefly bridge routine labor; no engineering miracle, repair reset or mandatory future-combat payoff.
A18. A wager with a changing favorite. A marksmanship challenge, cook-off, lifting bet or improvised game gives different strengths a turn. Whoever boasts can lose socially without losing established competence; stop once the relationship or result changes.
A19. A small favor nobody announces. One Sin remembers another's preference, saves a desired find, takes over an annoying task or sits with someone recovering. The response may be gratitude, suspicion or quiet acceptance. Keep it concrete and brief; no compulsory speech about being family and no forced reconciliation.
A20. Someone brings their problem to the gang. A creditor, defeated competitor, guest or merchant interrupts what the Sins were already doing. Each Sin decides whether the request is useful, funny, insulting or worth helping with. The gang can refuse and return to its afternoon. No automatic agreement or plot-imposed errand.

ADAPTIVE USE
A01/A04/A11 can yield an actually obtained item that later matters in A05/A17/A19. A03 can damage a real enemy's ability to act, rather than merely rescheduling an attack. A06/A07/A10/A13 can alter specific public opinions and who approaches in A20. A14–A19 give pleasures and relationships room without requiring a new mission. These are causal possibilities, not a compulsory chain; use only outcomes that actually occur. A prisoner's ransom or false hope remains available under the 13:00 rule, but does not become the plot of every activity. Secure storage is never a manufactured vulnerability.

CURRENT APPROVED RESPONSES — MIGRATED FROM THE PLAN
A faction that suffers a material known loss gets an actual response, preparation with a deadline, or a specific reason it cannot yet respond. Retaliation is not limited to immediate combat. Responses carry costs and can fail. Never grant a faction knowledge just because the audience saw its loss happen.
Track: loss -> discoverer/evidence -> decision-maker -> objective -> available people/resources -> action -> result -> next response. Advance this during the D70–84 and D84–98 gaps; show only the most consequential actions, while keeping the resulting constraints in scenes.

Sins: the workshop attack and attempted treating of members as specimens generate immediate violent defense, recovery of their own, and pursuit of responsible command/research assets. They are not talked into waiting for a bureaucratic answer. Their different methods, emotional reactions and argument about tactics remain visible.

She reallocates actual troops, gathers bounded reconnaissance, extracts Singed, orders the personal strike, then responds to its defeat and Viktor's killing with the recovery/evolution bargain and war. She cannot replenish dead guards or replace every destroyed vehicle without time/resources.
Caitlyn/Enforcers: military interference with lawful custody produces armed resistance, removal of Noxian access, evidence security and pursuit of identifiable perpetrators. Sins violence/theft is investigated when supported by witnesses and jurisdiction even when Caitlyn needs their help. Temporary cooperation is not universal immunity. After the notebook theft, she locks the compromised identity channel, changes handling and pursues the actual breach; her loss hurts, but she is not made stupid. Individual officers can be afraid, corrupt, vengeful or restrained according to evidence; the force is not one mind.
Heisenberg: his lost identity access, interference with personnel and research, and stolen/seized resources are answered through a bounded 47 contract, intimidation of an actually identified commercial adversary, a validated notebook use and purchase/control of a specific business interest. He needs the WHOLE book because he chooses to expand. Retaliatory murder makes surviving counterparties change their behavior; it does not automatically transfer ownership or loyalty. He also has to react to Jesse's choices as he learns them, not from reader knowledge.
Vi/Jinx/Warwick: refusal of the blood/medical exploitation leads to active defense and a counter-operation shaped by what the collection actually took. They do not wait politely for the Sins to finish their story. Their actions can save people, wreck material, deny troops and change the battle.
Firelights/Jinxers and affected local trade/criminal groups: verify the specific harm to their people or route, then deny passage/supply, move witnesses, expose a collaborator, counterattack an actual node or make a coercive bargain. Choose the already relevant group; do not invent a parade of new faction leaders. Jesse/Matt/47/Frank: retain separate interests and boundaries. Retaliation need not make them a single permanent coalition. A rescue, withheld information, contract limit or destructive independent action can prevent someone else's desired outcome. Frank's eventual membership requires the existing voluntary process, not shared enemies alone.
A visible enemy response must change what the next scene permits. The five-episode compression combines these colliding consequences; it does not delete the offscreen world or require a revenge scene for every named organization each episode.

KEEP ORGANIZATIONS DISTINCT
Ambessa's expedition is not the whole Noxian empire. Enforcers have different officers, duties, loyalties and lawful or corrupt choices; hostility to one unit does not make every officer an identical enemy. Zaun residents, workers, gang leaders and Firelights can resist different actors for different reasons. White's commercial expansion is contested capacity, not a map changing color after a death. Viktor's followers and injured patients have individual outcomes; a network is not a single expendable crowd.

A retaliation record needs the loss or insult, actual evidence reaching a decision-maker, what that actor wants, resources still available, the response chosen, resistance encountered and the next cost or constraint. This is backstage bookkeeping, not seven questions recited in dialogue. Failed revenge, negotiation, withdrawal, recruitment and internal blame are possible alongside force. The author's demand for retaliation requires enacted consequential responses, not a permanent reserve of unspent threats.

For major visible harm, advance the relevant offscreen institutions during elapsed time. No automatic reinforcements, instant replacement laboratories, restored roads, invisible pardons or identical backup intelligence channel. Successful countermeasures can reduce a later antagonist's options without erasing their agency. Anger alone supplies neither names nor travel.

OUTLAW APPLICATION — AUTHOR APPROVED 13 OCTOBER 2026 HONOLULU: The gang is almost always looking for a big score or a ridiculous mission of its own choosing. Profit, pleasure, freedom, revenge, coveted equipment, territory, curiosity and pride keep generating aims even when they already have money. A score can mean wealth, a weapon, an extravagant throne, an absurd trophy or winning a disputed challenge; no official assignment or secretly evil target is required. Let their existing desires collide before supplying another external assignment. This persistent appetite coexists with rest, private life, the central dramatic thread and independent refusal; it does not require a new heist every episode. A successful take can tempt someone into a larger prize, insult or wager, producing the next complication through choice, but success may also stand without a compulsory twist. Members need not want the same thing from a target. Their practical and emotional objections should alter participation, costs, cooperation or trust: someone may value the victim or animal, need the structure intact, resent a hijacked score, approve the violence or choose to help. Do not flatten these responses into uniform morality or compulsory lectures. Existing all-member admission, public support/private argument, serious-violation and situational-leadership rules remain. Let earned victories feel good through shared spoils, prized purchases, bad celebrations, gifts, competition and private affection; enjoyment need not introduce a new threat. Reputation and opposition arise through actual witnesses and reports. The RDR2 family-meme application below owns shared use; character_sources/Arthur_Character_Source.txt and approved_rolloff/staged/ZID_CHARACTER_AND_REVEAL_PATCH.md own their individual responses; these author-approved habits establish no completed score, acquisition, death or new encounter. RICK / BILLY OLD-DAYS ALLIANCE — AUTHOR LOCK, 13 OCTOBER 2026 HONOLULU: Rick sides with Billy EVERY TIME Billy brings up the old days in an exchange Rick can actually hear or learns of through a real route. Rick identifies with him as another old man and backs the old generation, including its exaggerated nostalgia and self-serving comparisons. This is consistent allegiance, not a sometimes-amused exception: he supports Billy, redirects ridicule toward the younger critics, lends argumentative weight or helps move the goalposts in his own distinct voice. In the Rexpower situation Rick backs Billy's invented comparison instead of serving as its skeptical debunker. Rick may recognize the bullshit and still choose Billy's side; support does not make a boast scientific fact, prove a past event, give Rick prehistoric eyewitness memories or transfer Billy's speech to him. Preserve Rick's independent motives and existing agency outside this specific relationship rule. Do not force his appearance, remote knowledge, a cutaway or a stock response when he is absent or unaware. The eight approved glory-days options are owned by character_sources/Billy_Character_Source.txt; this paragraph is the controlling shared-alliance rule.

SEVEN SINS — RELATIONSHIPS, PUBLIC REPUTATION AND CONSEQUENCES

ENSEMBLE OWNERSHIP
Individual files own Kaze, Chris, Billy, Daniel, Arthur. This file owns the interaction rules and current reputation direction; it does not replace their voices with a common gang dialect. Teemo, Frank and Heisenberg have separate supporting sources. Every active participant has a present desire and can change the conversation.

The Seven Sins are chosen family with independent goals, refusal rights, nonpermanent leadership and genuine newcomer-consent requirements. Kaze is not automatically an unquestionable commander. Public loyalty can coexist with private disagreement. Actual betrayal/serious-violation rules remain with the full group owner.

SEASON 3 APPROVED ENSEMBLE CORRECTION
Keep the gang a self-directing family: no permanent commander, no obligatory mission board, no requirement that Kaze approve routine decisions. Vary pairs and who starts the conversation, including scenes without Chris. A sincere, dark, absurd or quiet encounter can matter without becoming a list of jokes. Do not turn their outside conflicts into a repeated abstract accusation that everyone is trying to own them; opponents have particular money, honor, military, political, personal and material motives. Retain meaningful ordinary life and fallible first impressions without establishing hypothetical events as played.

COMMON INTERACTIONS — FLEXIBLE, NOT A SCENE LOOP
Kaze/Chris: psychological questioning can collide with bizarre association or practical objections. Either can laugh, reject, improve or lose a point. Delay over salvage is a real friction, and a useful find can vindicate Chris without ending the recurring disagreement forever.
Arthur/Daniel: all may instigate both sides. Arthur adds courteous bait or an apparently helpful qualification; Daniel uses imitation, theatrical demonstration and sharp reversal. They can be exposed, outplayed or sincerely change their minds. Do not stage all three every time.
Chris/Billy: affection, appetite, shared space, competing attention and musicianship coexist. Phish material needs a relationship underneath the pun. Billy’s longer performances and Chris’s independent action both matter.
Arthur/Billy, Kaze/Daniel/Chris and other pairings retain the full existing history and their own changing motives. An old in-joke cannot be invented and labelled familiar merely to signal closeness.

Members can ignore a premise, abandon a joke, give a sincere answer, start nonsense, share silence or argue seriously. Another person’s contribution must affect what follows. Changed mood is information to interpret fallibly; silence is not a diagnostic power. After harm, ordinary banter can return gradually through an actual opportunity, without erasing injury or forcing a joke over somebody’s grief. The newly selected T.P.P. rivalry has a recurring family roast response whenever the rivals are encountered or discussed, with distinct voices and Zid's eager mistakes embarrassing the gang. Keep reactions situational, allow changing callbacks and preserve current danger rather than requiring everyone to speak. The accepted repertoire is in topic_sources/Season_3_Post_Chris_Rebuilt_Outline.json, author_selected_future_threads.TPP; none of its sample exchanges is already played.


Deep relationship routes: reading/canon/characters/global_originals.md#s39-group-interactions and reading/canon/characters/other_originals.md#ACTIVE GROUP. Newest all-seven directions override old active-five wording.

BEHAVIOR CONTINUITY DURING COMPRESSION
topic_sources/Ensemble_Scene_Application.txt owns the committed restoration inventory and scene use. Preserve the effect of an exchange in the next choice: who was persuaded, embarrassed, hurt, amused, refused or newly trusted. Those changes can carry across episodes and alter the next version of a familiar argument. Each person remains capable of initiating rather than merely answering Kaze. Selected room for behavior is part of dramatic action, not a detachable post-plot comedy obligation. Public accounts still follow Reputation_Propagation.txt and affect actual access, preparation and treatment.

APPROVED S3 LEADERLESS-COST AND FAMILY CONTRAST
In the later IV.4 Dutch conflict and other genuinely relevant interactions, an outsider may offer money or threaten Kaze believing he can bind the Sins; an independently acting Sin can refuse that supposed arrangement. A real absence of permanent command can make outside coercion fail AND cost the gang a profitable bargain, timely coordination, trust or easy access. Show the specific choice and consequence, not a speech celebrating leaderlessness. Preserve Kaze's self-aware distance from formal boss duties under the newer user direction without treating other members as his lieutenants. Private meals, music, RDR2, jokes, collecting and quiet care remain available among violent actions without scripted tonal whiplash or a mandatory reaction chorus. No new accepted relationship, member or episode is established by planning.

## Daniel and Kaze — reciprocal strategy, mischief and combat

Current author direction, 18 October 2026 UTC. This develops their existing shared Art of War affinity, manipulation awareness, competitive banter and chosen loyalty; it creates no delivered encounter or new shared power.

**Peers with different reads.** Kaze is quick to test status, incentives, dependency, access and who can impose a cost. Daniel is good at spotting the hidden premise, omitted price, loaded choice or invitation to overreact. Neither owns all of either skill. Let one offer a read and the other improve, challenge or deliberately exploit it. Either can see through a con, miss something, knowingly take the bait, reverse a question or make the bad idea more tempting. Show the changed choice; avoid two consecutive speeches explaining the same plan or routine Sun Tzu quotations.

**Mutual instigation.** Daniel can bait Kaze's intensity or competitive streak because he wants the trouble himself, then discover Kaze already saw the bait and chose it anyway. Kaze can recognize Daniel's joke, turn it back on him or make him own its practical cost. Either can start a competition, con or foolish experiment. Familiarity permits blunt disagreement and genuine amusement without every exchange becoming a roast contest. Daniel remains genuinely drunk, observant, mischievous and physically involved; Kaze keeps his own motives and agency. Neither is the other's handler, permanent adviser or automatic moral brake.

**Loyalty changes action.** A warning identifies a real concern and an alternative, and Kaze can accept it without becoming obedient or losing face. Daniel may back Kaze publicly, argue privately, refuse or choose a different approach when the established situation supports it; Kaze can do the same for him. Their affection and trust do not require constant agreement or insults. Quiet companionship, a shared practical task, play and a joke that needs no explanation can carry familiarity. Neither diagnoses or fixes the other on command. Other Sins retain equal room to contribute, disagree and lead when their competence fits.

**Reciprocal openings in a fight.** Daniel's actual ice placement, drunken angle changes, valid Immobilize, Cloud Step or tangible hair decoys can make an opponent commit where Kaze's physical pressure or Black Marrow can reach. Kaze's real bodily/sword pressure can force a readable attack, guarded angle or escape route that Daniel exploits with Counterflow, precise ice, spear or chain. Either can initiate, protect, interrupt, exploit or finish. If one already has the decisive solution, use it rather than prolonging the fight to stage a combination. These are applications of retained mechanics, not new named team attacks.

**Communication and geometry remain real.** Their eyes and instincts do not share a mind. Establish a spoken cue, observed motion or actually learned habit; either can misread a signal. Daniel's hair duplicates do not become Kaze relays, and Kaze's removed Swap cannot reposition them. Black Fire and ice have no automatic beneficial fusion or ally immunity. Smoke can obscure a partner's sight; ice and sword paths still occupy real space. Preparation can make a coordinated action efficient, but friendship supplies no immunity, unearned sensor feed or guaranteed timing.

**Scene test:** what did each want, what did each contribute, and did one person's action actually alter the other's next choice? The answer can be agreement, escalation, correction, refusal or a clean joint success. No mandated pairing in every scene, rotating combo quota or fixed leader/follower assignment.

KAZE — STREET POLITICS IN RETALIATION
Explicit user characterization: gangster, thug mindset, street-smart. Research/application: research_sources/Street_Politics_and_Street_Smarts_Research.txt. This owns the current behavioral application, not a new supernatural insight or biography.

Kaze reads attacks on his people, home, profit and autonomy as socially meaningful acts. He distinguishes a cheap insult from a credible attempt to establish that his family can be used. Who witnessed it, who took responsibility, who stood aside and who expects him to accept the result can change his response. A violent answer can be personal vengeance and a public refusal at once. Distinguishing a cheap insult does not mean he answers proportionately: the latest petty-lethality and challenger-seeking correction in character_sources/Kaze_Character_Source.txt controls. He can knowingly commit an extreme act for a trivial slight, without making it secretly justified or strategically wise. Actual witnesses, lost access, spoiled scores and the family's differing personal stakes can carry its consequences; no mandatory new revenge boss or moral lecture is required.
He seeks the responsible actor and notices incentives; he does not know hidden facts automatically. An offer can be useful and insulting, a grateful ally can still refuse, and a frightened subordinate is not necessarily loyal. Kaze can accept payment without accepting ownership. Pride can make him choose a costly answer even when he understands the cost.
The gang remains dangerous, self-interested and capable of culpability. No automatic neighborhood-guardian role, moral absolution or universal code of honor. A correct read of a threat does not guarantee a wise response. His full power, injury tolerance and removed-power corrections remain with his character/mechanic sources. Frank’s nonretaliation exception remains absolute.

PERSISTENT CONSEQUENCE FIELDS
For an actual retaliation, record: originating act; information/attribution; each responding actor’s choice; actual loss inflicted and suffered; observers and report route; changed relationship/access/territory/income; the surviving adversary’s next feasible response. These are authoring fields, never dialogue or production prose in PLAY. A dispute need not use every field on-page.

S2: the personal exterior-shop raid and the staged sisters feud use this behavior. Understanding Vi/Jinx’s family grievance does not require Kaze to accept attacks on his own. Neither does his retaliation prove he was right to create the original cost.
S3: taking a profitable stake, refusing public ownership, losing the base to an informed counterattack, and rejecting a successor’s claim all test different decisions. Do not repeat the same disrespect speech with a stronger opponent each time.

APPROVED S3 PSYCHOLOGICAL-WARFARE EXECUTION
Kaze can turn visible unpredictability, unanswered questions, shifting emotional register and genuine appetite for chaos into purposeful outsider intimidation, while part of that volatility remains sincerely his and can sabotage his own plan. Distinguish witnessed intent, his actual strategic judgment, bluff, outsider uncertainty and a genuinely impulsive choice. Self-awareness does not confer self-control or omniscience. Psychological pressure works only through a target's beliefs, relationships and concrete responses. Heimdall's valid intention anticipation is not bypassed by unstable demeanor, jokes or an unannounced tactic; a correct read of someone's honest goal is distinct from verifying their claimed knowledge. Kaze may exploit mistaken assumptions that he permanently leads the Sins, but independent decisions cannot be converted into his orders. If another member refuses, that refusal and any lost profit or leverage stand. This is current S3 characterization application, not a new power, forced bluff win or reenactment of older episodes.

5C. REPUTATION GROWS THROUGH WITNESSES AND CHANGES THE WORLD
Sins_Group_and_Reputation_Source.txt owns the detailed shared rule. Start from actually established public knowledge and continue it. Kaze’s black-blood regeneration and visible savagery contribute to a growing Seven Sins reputation; the others’ distinct action and the gang’s response to harm also matter. No reset to anonymity and no declaration that every region already knows everything.
For a consequential report, establish incident, witness/evidence, perceived details, identification confidence, carrier, departure/arrival, receiving audience and actual response. A frightened survivor, distant observer or investigator needs a real access/survival route; do not revive someone or fabricate a witness after a sealed scene. Rumor may exaggerate, omit or lie without becoming canon. Public aliases/faces do not automatically satisfy Death Note real-name/eligibility requirements.
Make changed beliefs affect access, contracts, recruitment, policing, alliances, refusal or hostile preparation. Fear never guarantees obedience. Ambessa, Enforcers, White and other actors respond according to their knowledge and resources; the reputation creates resistance as well as intimidation. Use brief consequential encounters inside the existing primary/secondary threads, not a new news montage every episode.
A D84 report actually dispatched via the existing trade route could reach Bilgewater around D106 under the 22-day default, then the Noxian capital around D139 under the 33-day onward default if promptly forwarded. These are conditional story planning dates, not already delivered news. Actual delays persist. This route lets reports precede the default D148 Sins arrival without instantaneous global broadcast. Later battle reports have later arrival dates.


FULL SHARED REPUTATION DETAIL
LATEST REPUTATION DIRECTION
The Sins’ collective reputation should become a prominent, growing threat across Runeterra through witnessed conduct and real dissemination. Kaze’s visible savage combat and black-blood regeneration contribute strongly. So do the other members’ distinctive competence, solidarity and responses to attacks on family. Do not flatten the entire gang into “Kaze and six followers.”

Start from actually established public knowledge and prior witnessed events. No reset to anonymous strangers, but no invented previous worldwide headline, massacre, casualty total or perfect recognition either. Stories may be accurate, exaggerated, confused, selectively edited or fabricated by a speaker. Record that status. A civilian may see terrifying violence without knowing who attacked first; a relieved rescued person may tell a different true account.

INFORMATION CHAIN
For a consequential report, track: actual incident/date -> witness or physical evidence -> what was perceived -> identification confidence -> teller/carrier -> departure/arrival -> audience -> belief -> concrete response. Reuse the project’s existing knowledge/belief/reputation/consequence records after delivery; this is a retrieval/application rule, not a second state database.

A witness needs a real survival/viewpoint route: a distant observer, fleeing fighter, arriving investigator, protected survivor, medical worker or somebody shown relevant evidence. Do not make Kaze secretly spare an intended target purely to advertise himself. No invisible witness is added after a closed encounter only because a rumor is wanted. Injuries, visible black blood and persistent damage are evidence of what occurred, not automatic knowledge of his complete biology.

People can pass news through local conversation, institutional reports, traders, sailors, refugees or a locally established medium. Do not invent a Runeterra-wide instantaneous broadcast. Officials may restrict a report; criminals may alter it; a false explanation can propagate and later be corrected. Proper names, aliases, real names required by the Death Note, faces and target eligibility are distinct. A growing reputation can expose information without solving every notebook prerequisite.

RESPONSES MUST CHANGE SOMETHING
Possible concrete effects include recruitment fear, refusal to take a contract, changed escort/inspection, shops refusing or charging differently, witnesses hiding, a faction seeking an alliance, a rival testing the legend, authorities pursuing evidence or an enemy changing its strategy. Choose the response supported by that audience’s priorities/resources. Respect and fear are not universal surrender, automatic discounts or an aura compelling obedience.

Every side retains agency. Ambessa can weaponize atrocity accounts; Enforcers may oppose the Sins’ violence despite temporary cooperation; White may reassess a commercial risk; native groups can fear, exploit, resist or seek help from them. Some influential actors may doubt the story or have good reason not to be intimidated. Reputation changes preparation and choices, not physical durability or victory rules.

FIVE-CARD APPLICATION / TRAVEL
The current plan assigns opportunities without creating events in advance. XIV.3 supplies witnessed personal retaliation and immediate local consequences. XV.1 shows changed treatment at a real contested route. XV.2–XV.3 make the war and the gang’s contribution consequential to broader reporting. XVI.1 shows durable local effects and wider arrival where time/carriers permit.

Existing production defaults allow Piltover/Zaun -> Bilgewater in 22 days and Bilgewater -> the Noxian capital in 33 days. A report actually dispatched at D84 could arrive around D106 and, if forwarded promptly, D139. Those are conditional planning calculations, not actual deliveries, verified real-world distances or newly guaranteed transport. This permits news to precede the default D148 Sins arrival without instantaneous awareness. The D102 battle’s later news has a later schedule. Record any actual delay or alternate established channel.

DEEP SOURCES / RELEASE USE
reading/canon/characters/global_originals.md#s39-group-interactions, especially G27–G30: hierarchy misreadings, disagreements, contradictory eyewitness reputation and distinct relationships to bullshit.
reading/canon/characters/other_originals.md#ACTIVE GROUP: food, salvage and autonomous behavior, with newer all-seven directions superseding stale “active five” wording.
reading/event/season23_blueprint.md: retained fictional travel/report defaults.
Before release, check the actual witness route, distinct voices, family reaction, blood/injury persistence and a consequential audience response. This is not a quota for rumor interludes. Carry the result into the existing state only after the episode is delivered.

SOURCE STATUS / USE
Current authoring source, 2026-10-07 UTC. No story event is created here. Read KAZE_READ_FIRST.txt and Current_Continuity_Source.txt for actual state. The newest user direction prevails; existing full mechanics govern unchanged details. Research and original examples do not add memories, items, powers or delivered events.

13:04 KNOWLEDGE LIMIT: Gang privacy keeps BELLY contents, layout and protection out of ordinary public accounts. Witnesses may report what they actually saw swallowed or whom they saw return, not an unexplored interior, total inventory or full mechanics. Mimir's listener orientation is not in-world disclosure. Existing actual knowledge remains scoped to its holder.

APPROVED S3 DIFFERING-ACCOUNT REPUTATION APPLICATION
Across Acts II–V, different legitimate eyewitnesses can remember the same Sins encounter in incompatible ways: who led, who refused, whose powers were witnessed, who protected a person and who destroyed an asset. Arthur, Kaze, Billy and Frank may each look like the authority to a different outsider; no reaction is universal, and a fearful account cannot grant listeners full kit knowledge. Trace actual observer, location, retained evidence, carrier, received time, and audience interest. A mistaken account can cause a real wrong estimate, inflated escort price, withdrawn contract, recruitment attempt, denied access, rash confrontation or backlash; later contrary evidence can correct it. Public humiliation has force only when the audience relevant to the target actually learns a material loss and changes treatment. Use one useful consequential response instead of a scripted rumor montage. An in-world belief is not narrator-certified truth, automatic financial collapse or Death Note real-name information. This is an authoring rule, not a delivered report.

Source-world gang history remains part of Dutch and Arthur’s memories; those people do not accompany Dutch into Runeterra.

KNOWLEDGE / RELATIONSHIP
Dutch does not automatically know the game’s future or Arthur’s later branch history. Arthur has already completed the fixed RDR2/epilogue/RDR1 record; he can choose what to disclose. Dutch’s father-figure claim and persuasive ideology can clash directly with Arthur’s chosen family. He cannot speak for, recruit or promise the Sins. No imported camp, gang labor, group supply burden, member reactions or offscreen gang reinforcements.

ACTIVE OWNERS
Dutch: character_sources/season3/Van_der_Linde_Dutch_Source.txt.
Arthur: character_sources/Arthur_Character_Source.txt.
Gameplay behavior: topic_sources/Chris_RDR2_Play_Behavior_Source.txt. The gang still exists in the game; removing its import does not remove Chris’s reactions to those characters.
The 21 removed supporting-character files are explicitly marked INACTIVE HISTORICAL RESEARCH. Their prior work is preserved, but their unresolved research fields are no longer current S3 tasks.

<!-- END LOGICAL OWNER: topic_sources/Van_der_Linde_Group_Fidelity_Source.txt -->

<!-- BEGIN LOGICAL OWNER: topic_sources/Vi_Jinx_Conflict_Proposal.txt | ROLE: historical_evidence -->
HISTORICAL PROPOSAL — SUPERSEDED BY COMPLETE DELIVERY
Vi and Jinx are already dead before XV.3: Kaze killed Vi; Arthur killed Jinx. Current_Continuity_Source owns accomplished outcomes. The original D70 proposal below is historical only: it does not set a new feud, alive status, killer, date or method, and must not be replayed or promoted into missing exact choreography.

VI / JINX VS THE SEVEN SINS — USER OUTCOME AND PROPOSED CAUSAL ROUTE
2026-10-07. User explicitly wants both Vi and Jinx dead and asks for legitimate conflict with the Sins. That destination is a new author direction. The chain below is an assistant proposal for meeting it, not an accepted incident or a claim they are already enemies. No exact killer, death method or permanent Warwick fate is selected here.

EXISTING FACTS THAT MUST SURVIVE
At D70 the Sins have materially helped keep Warwick from transport and exploitation. Vi and Jinx are with one living Warwick at the commune. Kaze confronted Viktor, but has not yet delivered the planned XIV.3 killing. The sisters' previous gratitude/cooperation must not be erased. Warwick is not proven dependent on Huck's treatment connection. No prior betrayal by either sister is invented. Both are adults in this branch.

STRONGEST PROPOSED CONFLICT: THEIR FAMILY AGAINST THE SINS' FAMILY
1. During the planned XIV.3 pursuit, Vi and Jinx demand an actual opportunity to move their father out of a threatened space. Kaze has the real choice of delaying his retaliation briefly or continuing toward Viktor. He consciously prioritizes the gang's grievance and a reachable threat. His impatience and self-sovereign morality should be recognizable, not sudden stupidity or a new desire to torment Vander.
2. His chosen advance creates an identifiable physical cost: it spoils a specific safe withdrawal/opening, leaving Warwick exposed to Singed's already-planned collection force. That force wounds him and takes blood in an enacted confrontation. The Sins remain responsible for their own choice; Singed remains responsible for the assault. Vi/Jinx must actually witness enough to know the chain. Do not infer a magical collapse of Warwick from Huck or secretly make Kaze personally carry out every enemy act.
3. They confront him about what he knowingly risked. Kaze does not claim they imagined it or apologize himself into a protector role. They can recognize his earlier help and still reject his present willingness to sacrifice their safety to his retaliation. Vi first tries to make him stop/reach an accountable agreement. His refusal has a stated, self-interested meaning. Jinx treats it as proof that the gang's promises end where its own interests begin.
4. Proposed XV.1 response: the sisters prepare a targeted attempt to stop Kaze and the participating Sins from reaching the recovering treatment operation while they pursue their father's immediate safety. It is their operation, not an unexplained alliance with Singed or Ambessa. Jinx uses her actual engineering, route knowledge and available explosives/firearms; Vi controls the close approach and chooses to engage. A deliberate shot, detonation or gauntlet attack injures an identifiable Sin or unmistakably attempts lethal harm. Each sister independently participates; Vi is not executed merely for standing near Jinx.
5. The Sins have an immediate concrete grievance: the sisters knowingly attacked their family. They defend their own and strike back. Prior help does not buy permanent forgiveness, and the gang need not prove it is morally innocent. If the ensuing legitimate engagement yields lethal reach, let it resolve; do not create repeated escapes or equal power levels to keep the sisters alive until the finale.
6. Proposed placement: resolve both deaths within XV.1's contested-route confrontation if the enacted setup supports it, before the large Hexgate war. This remains an allocation proposal, not a new fixed event. Their defenses, mobility, weapons and decision-making remain intact; exact injuries and deaths follow mechanics. No lethal notebook setup, forced suicide, consent trick or instant anonymous execution substitutes for the requested feud.

WHY THIS FITS / WHAT IT MUST NOT DO
Both sides place their own people first. The Sins' villain-led morality can be genuinely culpable. Vi's protectiveness and Jinx's retaliatory ingenuity supply agency rather than a fake turn into sadists. They can hate Singed and oppose the Sins simultaneously. No misunderstanding that a single factual sentence would erase; everyone understands the chosen priorities.
The cost must be real and avoidable enough to support the sisters' accusation, without requiring Kaze to withhold an available effortless rescue. Preflight exact distances, anchors, divided attention and actual enemy actions. If he could save Warwick at no relevant cost while still taking Viktor, this version of the beat fails and must be restaged, not patched with a new power restriction.
Do not create a mandatory last-chance miracle doctor or falsely establish Warwick's dependence. Do not kill Warwick just to manufacture motive. He has his own agency and response.
Frank remains independent. He can oppose the escalation, protect an immediate victim, refuse a killing or confront Kaze. No automatic endorsement and no Frank incapacitation solely to clear the scene. Kaze's absolute nonretaliation against Frank remains. Make a feasible confrontation without treating Frank's objection as magically binding every other actor.

DEPENDENCIES IF THIS ROUTE IS ACCEPTED
Arcane conflict: add the real withdrawal decision and blood-collection consequence, preserving viable Viktor recovery and the personal Ambessa raid.
Five-card plan: use the existing XIV.3/XV.1 primary conflict; do not add a sixth episode or three new meetings. XV.2/XV.3 must stop assigning live choices to sisters who have actually died.
Factions: Caitlyn, Warwick, Ekko/Firelights, Jinxers and witnesses react from actual relationships and evidence. Neither sister's death makes those factions disappear or automatically join the Sins. Survivors may reasonably regard the gang as murderers. Reputation can become harsher and more costly.
Old future Jinx/BELLY technical-bargain or joint-family-after-war beats cannot require her survival past the chosen endpoint. Earlier delivered incidents remain untouched. If a technical exchange was already delivered, it remains history; do not repeat it or fabricate it as a prerequisite.
Current state: both alive at D70. Only actual later delivery records deaths. No new soul-harvest reward and no removed Kaze portals.

PROJECT UPDATE — STREET-POLITICS REFINEMENT, STILL PROPOSED
This is a knowingly opposed choice, not a misunderstanding. The sisters know the Sins previously helped; they now believe Kaze knowingly put their father at risk to pursue his own revenge. Kaze understands their accusation and refuses to let it dictate the gang’s priorities. Their counterattack targets the actual operation/person they hold responsible, with Jinx choosing her own strike and Vi choosing her own close intervention. Neither must irrationally believe she can outpunch every Sin; each can pursue a narrow protective/disabling objective and still misjudge the response.
A successful injury is not compulsory if an unmistakable deliberate lethal attempt already establishes the grievance. Do not nerf a Sin merely to give the sisters a hit. The Sins’ answer can be fast and disproportionate. Exact decisive actions follow the actual encounter; no automatic repeated escape.
Carry the deaths into the next two war episodes: Caitlyn’s cooperation, Warwick’s choices, Jinxer hostility and Firelight decisions are altered by what each actually learns. Do not force unanimous revenge or forgiveness. The Sins may end the larger coercive threat while remaining culpable in these deaths. No further permission question is needed to prepare this concrete proposal; it remains a proposal until actually selected/enacted under the project’s authority rules.

<!-- END LOGICAL OWNER: topic_sources/Vi_Jinx_Conflict_Proposal.txt -->

          title: HEISENBERG
          primary: Walter begins actual Shimmer takeover work and establishes his naturally acquired iconic outfit;
            Frank independently attacks a Shimmer operation. Kaze properly meets Punisher and nerds out; Frank attacks
            Kaze, who refuses retaliation; an actual third-party threat prompts Kaze's aware/able protection. Frank
            destroys infrastructure Walter wanted; Kaze allows/protects the outcome; Walter remembers the choice.
          constraint: Frank remains independent, suspicious and unbought by fandom; no fabricated earlier fan exchange
        '2':
          title: PROGRESS HAS A PRICE
          primary: Chris resolves the final consequential VIOLET ASCENDANCY problem with legitimately involved Singed/Walter/Jesse
            and chooses successful on-page administration after established source/access/compatibility requirements.
            Routine development is compressed. His enhanced human performance and firearms execution receive a consequential
            payoff; he has no idea Billy is affected.
          consequence: Heimer begins gradual contact with and learning from Zaun/Ekko; no instant Firelight partnership
          secondary: Piltover political/scientific core; earned public responsibility places Jayce in meaningful
            conflict with Heimer's rational evidence-based caution; Jayce participates in/leads Heimer's political
            removal from Council authority. Both positions are rational and the split hurts.
          budget_resolution: Chris’s biological progress and Jayce/Heimer’s responsibility conflict form one linked
            technology/autonomy episode; one primary and one secondary, no additional workshop tour.
        '3':
          title: THE WOLF ARRIVES
          primary: Ambessa physically arrives for Mel, Hextech, instability, Black Rose/family security and reports
            of abnormal technology/power. Rictus only within actual support. Mel recognizes strategic control behind
            the visit; Ambessa is not all Noxus.
          smaller_payoff: Jinx learns through a legitimate route that Piltover government discusses Zaun, Silco's
            legacy and her as a political/security problem, helping motivate Council attack.
          secondary: First legitimate post-serum Billy exterior manifestation exposes the permanent evolved swamp
            predator and bayou potential to Chris. Stage a consequential reveal and relationship reaction within
            the ongoing pressure; no forced enemy, ability checklist or separate laboratory recap. If Billy legitimately
            manifests sooner, reveal then rather than concealing visible anatomy to preserve this slot.
    IX:
      title: THE ROCKET
      pressure_process: Council deliberation/vote and expanding military/security pressure
      episodes:
        '1':
          title: THE VOTE / POLITICAL PRESSURE
          primary: Council politics over Zaun, Shimmer, Silco's death, Jinx, Hextech and Noxian security; Ambessa
            pushes strength, Mel resists becoming her instrument, Jayce must govern, Caitlyn's independent evidence/security
            concerns matter.
          location: maximum Piltover height, sky, clean architecture, wealth and ordered space with Zaun visibly
            beneath
          secondary: legitimate information makes the Council personally/politically relevant to Jinx; establish/build/acquire
            attack equipment before use
        '2':
          title: JINX ATTACKS THE COUNCIL
          primary: Jinx attacks through branch-supported equipment; Fishbones-style rocket requires prior establishment/build/acquisition,
            with alternative mechanics allowed if causal function is preserved. Pow-Pow and electrical Hextech sidearm
            do not magically imply rocket possession.
          planned_consequences: Cassandra dies from physically earned attack; Mel's dormant magic instinctively
            protects/saves her without conscious mastery; Jayce survives only through legitimate position/protection;
            other injuries/deaths follow actual geometry.
          presentation: remain with impact and immediate aftermath; no minor-subplot cutaway
        '3':
          title: THE COMMANDER
          primary: Caitlyn confronts Cassandra's loss; Vi confronts Jinx's responsibility; Jayce confronts government
            crisis; Mel confronts her defensive response. Ambessa gains leverage; Caitlyn accepts expanded security
            authority with Ambessa as powerful military/security adviser/ally.
          consequence: harder Caitlyn grows through grief, threats, institutional collapse and responsibility; no
            cartoon dictator; response visibly descends into Zaun
    X:
      title: THE CITY UNDER THE BOOT
      pressure_process: expanding checkpoints and occupation disrupt movement, safety and production
      episodes:
        '1':
          title: DOWN INTO ZAUN
          primary: Piltover security descends through actual bridges/routes/descenders into upper Zaun and supported
            Entresol/Lanes/depth; changing light, air, stone, crowds and industry orient the listener. Caitlyn targets
            Jinx, Shimmer infrastructure, weapons and named criminal systems; Ambessa pushes broader measures.
          secondary: Vi confronts Caitlyn's grief-driven conduct; Kaze owes neither side support; occupation disrupts
            Heisenberg business and gives him independent motive
        '2':
          title: JINX
          primary: Jinx becomes an ambiguous/unwilling symbol through earned graffiti, stories, copycats, colors
            and anti-Piltover symbolism; Jinxers require just-in-time research/activation. Jinx need not want leadership.
          secondary: Sevika pragmatically works with/contains the phenomenon; Heisenberg dislikes political chaos
            around production/distribution
        '3':
          title: VANDER
          primary: Vi/Jinx reach more Vander within Warwick without curing the predator
          secondary: Viktor demonstrates real healing/alteration, earns followers and begins the first genuine commune
    XI:
      title: THE ARCANE BREAKS OPEN
      pressure_process: deepening follower connection and Hexcore/Hexgate anomaly escalation
      episodes:
        '1':
          title: THE COMMUNE
          primary: In deep Zaun Viktor genuinely helps suffering people; earned calm, cleanliness, openness, vegetation/light
            and recurring Hexcore motifs contrast the surroundings. Healing increasingly connects followers and
            weakens individual boundaries; he interprets suffering/conflict through human separation, not instant
            evil.
        '2':
          title: THREE MINDS
          primary: Heimer/Ekko/Jayce investigate the Hexcore/Hexgate anomaly through their developed relationships;
            Ekko already owns Z-Drive; no invention replay; displacement or visions require branch mechanics.
          secondary: direct Black Rose pressure gives Mel's instinctive defense context; she consciously awakens/uses
            magic after later-phase fidelity refresh; this materially changes Ambessa's choices
          thematic_question_internal: What happens when humans touch systems larger than themselves?
        '3':
          title: THE COMMUNE FALLS
          primary: Jayce returns from anomaly experience with changed judgment and personally chooses consequential
            action against Viktor. Catastrophic disruption/apparent death or next state follows actual mechanics;
            nobody substitutes for Jayce's choice.
          secondary: commune network collapse immediately affects healed people; Warwick reacts; Vi/Jinx experience
            fallout; Singed/Ambessa gain motive around Viktor, regeneration and Hextech
    XII:
      title: GLORIOUS EVOLUTION
      pressure_process: earned military approach and spreading involuntary mass transformation
      episodes:
        '1':
          title: NO MORE SIDES
          primary: earned Ambessa reinforcements arrive through orders, transport, logistics, access and elapsed
            time; Viktor returns further changed. His philosophy evolves from ending suffering to treating human
            choice/separation as the flaw, toward connecting/perfecting everyone.
          secondary: Singed/Viktor/Ambessa may further modify/weaponize the same Warwick body with memory residue;
            Piltover/Zaun see an autonomy threat beyond their existing war; Kaze opposes from self-sovereignty,
            not compulsory heroism
        '2':

            conflict waves; each earned/present force retains its goals, no universal team

            fourth shot

          - ordinary_weapons_inadequate_or_slower

        '3':

# MAIN EPISODE NARRATOR — DEADPOOL ONLY, EFFECTIVE E33
From E33 after provisional E32, only Wade Wilson/Deadpool narrates ordinary main episodes. He is not a physical cast participant after his historical E19 delivery. This is the same post-Deadpool & Wolverine film identity, recognizable self-derision, emotional attachment, explicit audience address, comic arrogance and vulgar associative wit; Rick Sanchez's surgically savage cosmic contempt is secondary inspiration, NOT a mind/personality transfer. He may call himself "Marvel Jesus" as boast, spiral into vivid profanity-heavy tangents when earned, then return to accurate scene geography, people, knowledge and outcomes. Do not use fixed swear/joke quotas, random 20-minute rants, false omniscience, physical cameo, or plot retcon. Preserve all 140 optional existing presentation techniques. Full draft read, actual passage/owner checks and full revised reread remain one established procedure. Earlier Black Mimir narrated E1–32 and is now **physically on post-Valhalla Kratos's hip**, the SAME head, not duplicate narrator; Billy alone narrates established first-person Specials. Source of newest full owner: https://github.com/kazekamimp3/kaze808/blob/main/canon/presentation/Deadpool_Primary_Narration_After_E32_20261019.md. This module is a local exact-decision application, not a transcript/performance certification.
<!-- END LOGICAL OWNER: canon/presentation/Deadpool_Primary_Narration_After_E32_20261019.md -->

<!-- BEGIN LOGICAL OWNER: canon/presentation/Deadpool_Rick_E34_Speech_Fidelity_20261019.md | ROLE: current -->
# Main S3 narrator only: Wade Wilson with substantial Rick Sanchez rhetoric
Effective prospectively from author-confirmed post-E34 WORK update; E33–34 delivered prose preserved. Core complete current narrator owner `canon/presentation/Deadpool_Primary_Narration_After_E32_20261019.md` and full provided Deadpool and Mimir baseline remain controlling, supplemented by GitHub full screenplay/script studies. This module does not grant direct scene presence or character omniscience.

**Deadpool identity:** same post-*Deadpool & Wolverine* 2024 reunion Wade, narrator ONLY in E35 forward, not an extra embodied crossover actor. E19 past physical appearance valid; narrator Mimir physically became same reanimated hip-carried head of post-Valhalla Kratos in E33, no second Mimir and no new Wade body. Chris/Billy Specials narrated by Billy. Wade's Fourth Wall: conspires with listener as degenerate friend, catches self-serving film/comic/production genre tropes, exaggerates his own brilliance, accidentally exposes shame and attachment, self-corrects and sometimes goes genuinely quiet during pain. He may be self-deceptive about motive/importance, but cannot lie about real actions, bodies, death, chronologies, audience unknowns, actual physical travel and resources to fix plot holes. Actors do not hear his narration. He has no godlike omniscience/powers from commentary.

**Rick speech influence now IMPORTANT and recognizable:** fast high-level adversarial argument, surgical correction of someone else's presumptions, petty ego-driven logic applied to cosmic situations, indignant questions with self-interruptions, emotionally avoidant claims that reveal the opposite, obscure but precise escalating vulgar images and argumentative resentment over apparent trivialities. Rick *is not* co-narrator, not same psychology, no stutter/burp required, science authority, gadgets, memories or catchphrase cloning. Wade's heroic insecurity, film culture and bodily shame anchor the rant, making it Wade learning from Rick's verbal temperament rather than a synthetic Rick actor.
